================================================================================
SECTION 7 — Architecture principles (derived from requirements)  [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Cross-cutting architecture principles. Detail lives in earlier functional
  sections; this suite verifies the principles still hold as a system.

  - Single codebase for Central + Shared hub(s) + all White-labels.
  - Three instance roles: central (control plane), shared (public/content),
    white_label (private client).
  - Roles: power_admin, finproms_admin, client_admin, network_admin, firm_admin,
    website_admin, top_management, accounts_admin, auditor, manager, approver,
    admin_staff, advisor, user. Multi-role UNION; all roles equal (§2.7.1).
    NA/FA firm heads + WTL site assign (§3.4 / §3.3d). Firm-scoped + Delegated
    compliance (§2.7.0a). Oversight vs multi-firm allowlist (§2.7.0 B).
  - Separate FE shells: member catalog (Shared/WL) + universal /my-dashboard
    (Capabilities + pa_* on Central). Legacy /client-admin + /power-admin →
    /my-dashboard.
  - Tenant/hub config in DB (branding, flags, rights).
  - Feature flags / rights checklists drive behaviour AND menus — not
    hard-coded forks. PA has own platform checklist on Central.
  - Central = configuration + remote-control source of truth (registry + remote
    DB creds); each content hub own DB. Shared is content hub only — not
    control plane.
  - Spawn Shared/WL by copy codebase to cPanel + register on Central.
  - Design for future: AI generation, in-app edit, compliance, Document
    Repository, more features.
  - UK GDPR tooling (§6E) phased per 6E.1.
  - Security Monitoring (§6F / NFR-SEC) distinct from 6A and 6C.
  - Settings that vary (e.g. NEW-banner duration) come from the database.

Actors:
  ENG = Engineering / architecture reviewer
  PA  = power_admin on Central
  OPS = Deploy / spawn ops

Layer tags: [PROCESS] [BE] [FE] [FE+BE]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-A  Single codebase + three instance roles
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-001 | [PROCESS] One codebase artefact serves Central, Shared, and
             White-label (no product forks)
Steps: Compare deploys / repo; hub.type from config/DB
Expected: Same code; behaviour via hub type + DB config (§4.1)

TC-7-002 | [BE] Three instance roles recognised: central | shared | white_label
Steps: Inspect Hub::TYPE_* / hub.type on each deploy
Expected: Exactly those control-plane vs content distinctions

TC-7-003 | [BE/FE] central = control plane; shared + white_label = content hubs
Steps: PA tools only on Central; member catalog on Shared/WL
Expected: Control-plane APIs/UI not exposed as control plane on Shared (§6)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-B  Roles + multi-role UNION + firm/oversight model
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-004 | [BE] Full role set present in product (power_admin … user list in
             summary)
Steps: Inspect role constants / matrix columns
Expected: Listed roles available (dormant columns OK until activated — §5.2)

TC-7-005 | [BE] User may hold MULTIPLE equal roles; effective caps = UNION;
             no primary/secondary (§2.7.1)
Steps: Assign two roles with disjoint caps; call gated API
Expected: UNION allows action; removing one role drops only that role’s caps

TC-7-006 | [BE] Network Admin / Firm Admin can designate Document Repository
             firm heads (§3.4) and request WTL sites / assign Website Admins
             (§3.3d) when caps/modules allow
Steps: Smoke paths
Expected: Architecture supports these responsibilities

TC-7-007 | [BE] Member-firm users firm-scoped + one-way Delegated compliance
             (§2.7.0a)
Steps: Firm A user cannot see Firm B; delegated access one-way as designed
Expected: Holds

TC-7-008 | [BE] Oversight Central/Network roles (network_admin / top_management /
             auditor / accounts_admin / approver) may manage/review hub-wide
             when capped; peer/non-oversight Central staff use multi-firm
             allowlist (§2.7.0 B)
Steps: Compare oversight vs allowlisted peer
Expected: Distinct access models

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-C  Frontend shells + legacy redirects
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-009 | [FE] Member catalog app on Shared / White-label separate from
             dashboard tools
Steps: Browse / vs /my-dashboard as member vs admin
Expected: Distinct shells/experiences

TC-7-010 | [FE] Universal Dashboard at /my-dashboard driven by Capabilities +
             (on Central) pa_* checklist
Steps: Toggle a matrix cap and a pa_* flag
Expected: Nav/tools appear/disappear accordingly — not hard-coded role pages

TC-7-011 | [FE] Legacy /client-admin and /power-admin redirect to /my-dashboard
Steps: Navigate both
Expected: Redirect (align §6)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-D  DB-driven config + checklists (no hard-coded forks)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-012 | [BE] Branding, functionality flags, modules, capabilities stored in
             database / hub config
Steps: Change branding + a Functionality flag + a matrix cell; reload
Expected: Behaviour follows DB without code deploy

TC-7-013 | [PROCESS/BE] Feature flags / rights checklists drive behaviour AND
             dashboard menus — not “if shared show X” hard-codes for
             matrix-driven features
Steps: Flip unusual Shared/WL defaults opposite of “usual”
Expected: Behaviour follows checklist (align §5.2)

TC-7-014 | [BE] Power Admin platform capability checklist separate from per-hub
             checklists; stored/managed on Central (§5.3)
Steps: Change pa_*; inspect content-hub DBs
Expected: Central platform Setting only

TC-7-015 | [BE] Variable settings (e.g. NEW-banner duration / new_banner_days)
             come from database settings — not constants alone
Steps: Change setting; observe banner window
Expected: DB value controls behaviour (§4.1 settings)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-E  Central source of truth + spawn model
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-016 | [BE] Central holds hub registry + remote DB credentials; each content
             hub has its own DB
Steps: Create WL; inspect Central registry vs remote content DB
Expected: Registry on Central; catalog data on hub DB (§1A / §4.1)

TC-7-017 | [BE/PROCESS] Shared Hub is NOT the control plane
Steps: Attempt registry/cross-hub control from Shared deploy
Expected: Denied / not offered (align §6 / §4.4)

TC-7-018 | [PROCESS] New Shared/WL spawned by copying same codebase to another
             cPanel (or host) + registering on Central with wiring
Steps: Ops spawn checklist
Expected: Copy code → own DB/.env → register + wire on Central (§1A)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-F  Design-for-future + GDPR + Security Monitoring placement
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-019 | [PROCESS] Architecture leaves room for deferred features (AI
             generation, in-app edit, further compliance/docs features) without
             requiring a second codebase
Steps: Review deferred items (§3.1/§3.2 DEFER) vs single-codebase principle
Expected: No fork required; flags/modules can gate future work

TC-7-020 | [PROCESS] UK GDPR tooling exists per §6E / phased §6E.1 (privacy,
             DSAR, anonymising erasure, retention)
Steps: Confirm GDPR page + Privacy gate present
Expected: Architecture includes privacy programme (not bolted as separate app)

TC-7-021 | [FE/PROCESS] Security Monitoring (§6F) is a separate functionality
             module from Activity Logs+anomalies (§6A) and compliance audit
             (§6C)
Steps: Nav comparison with flags on
Expected: Three distinct areas (align §6F / §6A)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7-G  System E2E / regression of principles
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-7-022 | [FE+BE] E2E architecture story: Central PA registers WL → sets
             Functionalities + Capabilities → wires remote DB → acts on hub →
             member uses catalog on WL deploy from same codebase → checklist
             change updates WL menus without code fork
Steps: Full path
Expected: Principles 1–8 demonstrated end-to-end

TC-7-023 | [PROCESS] Architecture review checklist signed: single codebase,
             three roles, checklist-driven UI, Central SoT, Shared not control
             plane, DB-driven settings
Steps: Review meeting / PR architecture note
Expected: Documented Pass against this section

================================================================================
SECTION 9.1 — NFR-SEC Regular security monitoring and updates accordingly
              [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Statement: Platform SHALL perform regular security monitoring and apply
  updates accordingly. Capable users SHALL view a detailed security monitoring
  report showing when a security event/breach-related incident occurred and
  what happened.

  Product fulfilment: Security Monitoring module (§3.3f / §6F) — event feed,
  incident lifecycle, aggregate report; gated by secmon_* when
  security_monitoring ON. Report detail MUST include: timestamp, severity,
  category, human description, actor/source where known, current status.

  Operational fulfilment:
  - Composer + npm monitored for vulns on regular cadence (CI audit and/or
    Dependabot/Renovate).
  - Critical/high triaged & patched within SLA (ops runbook; default critical
    ≤7 days, high ≤14 days, unless risk-accepted in writing).
  - Last scan / overdue status on SecMon Report tab when module enabled.
  - SECURITY HARDENING phases remain in force; this NFR adds continuous
    monitoring + update cadence (not a replacement for hardening).

  Acceptance (done):
  [x] flag + secmon_* gate UI/API
  [x] detectors failed-auth / rate-limit / blocked-login (min) with occurred_at
  [x] capable user report: when / what / status
  [x] incident acknowledge → investigate → resolve
  [x] scan-now / Monitor now (composer audit); no daily SecMon schedule
  [x] scan status visible after Monitor now
  [ ] CI Dependabot still optional
  [x] GDPR anonymisation on erase for security_events / incident notes

  Related but distinct: §6A, §6C, §3.3e, §6E.3, NFR-SEC-FW §9.5.

Cross-ref: Primary functional pack §3.3f + §6F; §8 open CI pipeline item.

Actors:
  VE/VR/MI = secmon caps
  OPS = dependency triage / SLA owner
  ENG = CI / Dependabot maintainer

Layer tags: [BE] [FE] [FE+BE] [PROCESS] [SEC]

================================================================================
SECTION 9.3 — NFR-GC-ENC Encryption of Generic Compliance data at rest
              [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Statement: GC free-text (description / reviewer feedback) SHALL be encrypted
  at rest so a database dump does not expose plaintext.

  Scope — text columns:
    general_compliance_request_versions.description
    general_compliance_request_versions.feedback
    general_compliance_request_versions.future_feedback

  Out of scope / deferred:
    - Attachment/supporting-file **bytes** on disk — attempted then REVERTED
      (broke cPanel/LiteSpeed downloads). Files stay public disk + public URLs.
      Revisit object-storage SSE later.
    - content_type, status, reviewer display name, request name

  Mechanism:
    - Text: SafeEncrypted cast (legacy plaintext readable until backfill)
    - Audit trail MUST NOT duplicate feedback plaintext in metadata (flags only)
    - Text search across encrypted body NOT supported; q uses non-encrypted
      fields only

  Ops:
    - php artisan security:encrypt-general-compliance [--dry-run]
    - Protect and back up APP_KEY

  Acceptance [x]: ciphertext for new/updated versions; API returns plaintext to
  authorised users; attachments public disk+URL; audit metadata no feedback
  body; documented in §3.3b + §8.

  Related but distinct: secret encryption (hardening); NFR-SEC §9.1; NFR-PRIV
  §6E; NFR-SEC-NAV §9.4.

PRIMARY SUITE (functional): §3.3b-G / 3.3b-H — TC-3.3b-035 … 043.
This §9.3 pack is the NFR acceptance / ops / scope guardrail layer.

Actors:
  G   = GC submitter / reviewer (authorised)
  QA  = Security / NFR verifier
  OPS = Deploy / APP_KEY custodian

Layer tags: [BE] [SEC] [PROCESS]

          limits, backfill command all mapped

Next: §9.4 NFR-SEC-NAV (below)
================================================================================

================================================================================
SECTION 9.4 — NFR-SEC-NAV External link / copied-URL / cross-site entry hardening
              [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Status: IMPLEMENTED. Product choice: SHOW ERROR (mode D) — do not open the
  target authenticated page on external entry (paste / email / chat / redirect
  from non-hub app). In-app SPA navigation continues normally.

  ALLOW: in-app nav / router / breadcrumbs; home `/` from outside (continuity
  so logged-in users can continue); guests on protected routes → `/login`
  (never show ERROR to guests).
  BLOCK (authenticated): first external entry to protected deep route → ERROR
  page; do not mount target; safe button to home (if member_view_site_pages)
  or dashboard.
  Allowlist: password reset, email verify, Stripe return, invite, public pages.
  Backend: SameSite strategy, CSRF, SafeFrontendRedirect, config
  security_navigation.php, optional POST /security/external-navigation-blocked
  → nav.external_entry_blocked when SecMon ON.
  Frontend: navigationContinuity (sessionStorage) + ProtectedRoute guard.

  Out of scope: unshareable URLs forever; stopping fake phishing domains;
  replacing Capabilities; breaking public pages.

Cross-ref: Sanctum SPA § hardening; §6F optional events; §6B-SESSIONS;
  NFR-SEC-FW §9.5 (not navigation).

Actors:
  A   = Authenticated user (any role with session)
  G   = Guest
  FE  = Frontend SPA
  BE  = Backend allowlist / redirect helpers

Layer tags: [FE] [BE] [FE+BE] [SEC] [PROCESS]

================================================================================
SECTION 9.5 — NFR-SEC-FW Firewall and malware protection  [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Status: IMPLEMENTED (app-layer IP deny + upload gates; Access restrictions
  Option B 2026-10-20). ClamAV OFF by default. Host/cPanel firewall = ops.
  Cross-refs: §6F events, §9.1, Access Control.docx (Option B subset).

  A) Application firewall — denied IPs (CIDR/hyphen ranges) via
     SECURITY_FIREWALL_DENIED_IPS / config; DenyBlockedIps → 403; optional
     auto-block on failed-login threshold; events fw.ip_denied /
     fw.ip_auto_blocked when SecMon ON.
  B) Malware / upload — ScanUploadedFiles on API multipart; reject dangerous
     extensions (+ embedded e.g. .php.jpg); reject SVG by default (incl.
     favicon); optional ClamAV (MALWARE_SCAN_*); events data.upload_rejected /
     data.malware_detected.
  C) Complementary: SecurityHeaders, rate limiters, per-route mime allowlists.
  D) Access restrictions Option B — security_monitoring ON +
     secmon_manage_access; /my-dashboard/access-control; hub + user rules;
     Monitor vs Enforce; Simulate; History/restore; self-lockout confirm;
     emergency unlock + artisan access-control:unlock; admin escape;
     TRUSTED_PROXIES / GEOIP_* hardening; blocked attempts feed (not
     activity_logs).

  Ops: host firewall/fail2ban; ClamAV defs if required; TRUSTED_PROXIES behind
  CF/LB; migrations on each hub DB.
  Out of scope: full WAF/IDS/SIEM; zero-malware guarantee; retroactive disk
  re-scan; full Access Control.docx platform features.

Feature tests named: SecurityFirewallTest, AccessRestrictionTest,
  GeoIpTrustHeadersTest. Also align TC-3.3f-035–038 for access gates.

Actors:
  X   = Denied / restricted client IP
  U   = Uploader
  AC  = Role WITH secmon_manage_access (+ security_monitoring ON)
  OPS = Host / ClamAV / proxy config

Layer tags: [BE] [FE] [FE+BE] [SEC] [PROCESS]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.5-A  Application firewall (A)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.5-001 | [BE] Denied IP (exact) receives HTTP 403 on API before controller
Steps: Set denied_ips; call API as that client
Expected: 403; controller not reached (SecurityFirewallTest)

TC-9.5-002 | [BE] CIDR and hyphen IP ranges (e.g. 203.0.113.10-203.0.113.50)
             deny matching clients
Steps: Configure range/CIDR; probe inside/outside
Expected: Inside denied; outside allowed

TC-9.5-003 | [BE] Optional auto-block after failed-login threshold when
             SECURITY_FIREWALL_AUTO_BLOCK=true
Steps: Enable; exceed threshold from IP
Expected: Subsequent API 403; fw.ip_auto_blocked when SecMon ON

Steps: Default config; fail logins
Expected: SecMon events may exist; IP not firewall-denied unless auto_block on

             security_monitoring ON
Steps: Trigger deny with SecMon on vs off
Expected: Events when on; deny still works when off

================================================================================
SECTION 9.6 — NFR-SEC-APP Protection against SQLi, XSS, CSRF, brute-force,
              malware, DDoS (app-layer) + SSRF  [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Status: IMPLEMENTED. Formalises hardening + SafeHtml, global API throttle,
  auth throttle keys. Volumetric DDoS = ops/CDN/host — Laravel throttles are
  app-layer abuse mitigation only.

  A) SQLi — Eloquent/bindings; no request concatenation into raw SQL
  B) XSS — CSP on API; SafeHtml on Terms/Privacy/Cookies; SPA DOMPurify;
     scriptable uploads via §9.5
  C) CSRF — Sanctum SPA cookies + CSRF; CORS FRONTEND_URL; §9.4 complements
  D) Brute-force — auth limiter IP(+email); OTP/reset throttles; SecMon +
     optional FW auto-block
  E) Malware — delivered by §9.5 (cross-ref)
  F) App-layer flood — global api limiter; imports/wc-publish/auth; Stripe
     webhook excluded; secmon api.rate_limited
  G) SSRF — SafeOutboundUrl on WC embed/cPanel/preview; cpanel_domain validate;
     hub-scoped embed auth (ticket/session) before remote DB
  H) Hub /internal/* purpose-scoped secrets (backup≠restore≠GC≠deploy-env)

Cross-ref: §9.1/9.4/9.5/9.7/9.8; SECURITY HARDENING; §6B purpose keys.

Actors:
  ATT = Attacker / abuse client
  U   = Legitimate user / SPA
  OPS = CDN / host firewall owner

Layer tags: [BE] [FE] [SEC] [PROCESS]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

             static fragments only
Steps: Grep raw SQL usage; review any with request input

Next: §9.7 NFR-BACKUP-ENC (below)

================================================================================
SECTION 9.7 — NFR-BACKUP-ENC Hub backup encryption at rest  [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Status: IMPLEMENTED. Functional detail in §6B; this NFR is the security
  statement for encrypted archives.

  Statement: Backup ZIPs SHALL be encrypted at rest so filesystem/hosting
  compromise does not expose plaintext SQL/files. Download/restore decrypt
  transparently for authorised operators with the correct key.

  Mechanism: Streaming AES-256-GCM, magic HBENC1 (HubBackupEncryptedArchive);
  HUB_BACKUP_ENCRYPT_AT_REST + HUB_BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY; create→zip→encrypt;
  receive preserves ciphertext; download/restore decrypt; hub_backups.is_encrypted;
  legacy plaintext still supported.

  Ops: Same shared key on Central + all content hubs; rotate carefully (v1 no
  multi-key ring); protect like APP_KEY; never commit.

  Acceptance [x]: new backups encrypted; download plaintext ZIP; restore
  encrypted+legacy; dual-store passes is_encrypted without double-encrypt;
  documented §6B+§8+9.7; tests cover round-trip.

PRIMARY SUITE: §6B-H / related — TC-6B-041 … 045 (+ download/restore cases
  TC-6B-023, 027–029, 051–052).

Actors:
  PA  = power_admin with pa_manage_hub_backups
  OPS = Key custodian across hubs

Layer tags: [BE] [SEC] [PROCESS]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.7-A  Reuse §6B encryption pack
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.7-001 | [PROCESS] Execute TC-6B-041–045 (+ download/restore encryption
             paths) as core NFR-BACKUP-ENC verification
Steps: Mark Pass/Fail on those cases
Expected: Core crypto covered without full re-authoring

TC-9.7-002 | [PROCESS] Traceability to §9.7 acceptance bullets
Steps: Map 6B cases → acceptance list
Expected: Encrypt, download ZIP, restore both, dual-store flag, docs/tests

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.7-B  Statement + mechanism
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.7-003 | [SEC/BE] On-disk backup file is HBENC1 / AES-256-GCM ciphertext —
             not a plaintext ZIP — when encrypt_at_rest on + key set
Steps: Create backup; read file magic / try unzip without decrypt
Expected: Encrypted; is_encrypted=true (align TC-6B-041)

TC-9.7-004 | [BE] Download API streams plaintext ZIP (PK…) to authorised PA —
             no HBENC1 at start of download body
Steps: GET download
Expected: Align TC-6B-023

TC-9.7-005 | [BE] Restore works for encrypted archives (shared key) and legacy
             plaintext archives
Steps: Restore each type
Expected: Both succeed (align TC-6B-044 + 027–029)

TC-9.7-006 | [BE] Dual-store: content hub upload to Central preserves
             is_encrypted; Central does not double-encrypt ciphertext
Steps: Remote backup → Central receive; inspect disk + flag
Expected: Single encryption layer; flag true

TC-9.7-007 | [BE] Without shared HUB_BACKUP_ENCRYPTION_KEY, APP_KEY-derived key
             is single-server only — cross-hub decrypt fails/unsupported
Steps: Align TC-6B-043
Expected: Documented limitation holds

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.7-C  Ops — key hygiene + rotation
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.7-008 | [PROCESS] Same base64 32-byte key configured on Central AND every
             content hub for dual-store / remote restore
Steps: Ops inventory of .env across deploys
Expected: Matching keys; round-trip works (align TC-6B-042)

TC-9.7-009 | [PROCESS] Key never committed to git; protected like APP_KEY
Steps: Search repo for committed encryption key material
Expected: Absent from VCS; only .env / secrets store

TC-9.7-010 | [PROCESS] Rotation: new key only after old archives pruned OR old
             key kept until retention clears — v1 has no multi-key ring
Steps: Ops rotation runbook
Expected: Procedure documented; no silent key swap stranding archives

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.7-D  Related distinct + GDPR residual
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.7-011 | [PROCESS] Distinct from NFR-GC-ENC (field crypto), NFR-AVAIL/§6B.1
             (DR), NFR-SEC-VAL (input validation)
Steps: Index
Expected: Clear separation

TC-9.7-012 | [PROCESS/BE] GDPR erasure does NOT rewrite historical backup dumps
             (residual risk) — NFR-PRIV / §6E
Steps: Align TC-6B-045 / TC-6E.2-003
Expected: Archives unchanged on erase

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.7-E  E2E
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.7-013 | [FE+BE] E2E: Backup now (encrypted) → list is_encrypted → download
             ZIP opens → restore encrypted copy → legacy plaintext still
             restores
Steps: Full path on Central (+ content hub dual-store if available)
Expected: Acceptance criteria Pass

================================================================================
SECTION 9.8 — NFR-SEC-VAL Server-side validation and sanitization of all user
              inputs  [DONE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Status: IMPLEMENTED. Complements NFR-SEC-APP (XSS) and NFR-SEC-FW (malware);
  does not replace them. Client checks MUST NOT be the only gate.

  Statement: Validate all user inputs on the server before business logic
  persists/acts; sanitize to remove dangerous control characters and, for rich
  text, disallowed HTML.

  A) Validation — every mutating API uses $request->validate (or helper) → 422;
     type/length/allowlists/file mimes; machine endpoints still auth+validate;
     list filters cast/allowlisted — never concat into SQL.
  B) Sanitization — SanitizeRequestInput strips null bytes/C0 controls;
     InputSanitizer::plain() / richHtml()→SafeHtml; PageContentDefaults
     allowlists; SafeFrontendRedirect; ScanUploadedFiles (§9.5).
  C) Frontend complementary — sanitizeRichText on submit/render; still 422
     from server.

Cross-ref: §9.6 XSS/SafeHtml; §9.5 uploads; §9.4 redirects.

Actors:
  U   = API / SPA client
  ATT = Malicious payload sender
  M   = Machine/internal caller

Layer tags: [BE] [FE] [SEC] [PROCESS]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.8-A  Validation on mutating APIs
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.8-001 | [BE] Representative POST/PUT/PATCH endpoints call validate() and
             return 422 with structured errors on failure
Steps: Omit required fields / wrong types on Terms, GC submit, settings, posts
Expected: 422 JSON errors; no partial unsafe persist

TC-9.8-002 | [BE] Rules enforce type, max length, Rule::in / exists, file
             mimes/max where applicable
Steps: Oversized string; invalid enum; bad file mime
Expected: 422

TC-9.8-003 | [BE] Body-bearing DELETE (if any) also validates when applicable
Steps: Identify such routes; send invalid body
Expected: Validated or explicitly body-ignored safely

TC-9.8-004 | [BE] Machine/internal endpoints authenticate AND validate file/
             payload shape (backup receive, Stripe webhook, GC file bridge)
Steps: Missing signature/key; invalid archive upload
Expected: 401/403 then 422 — not blind accept

TC-9.8-005 | [BE] List/query filters validated or cast to safe types — never
             concatenated into SQL
Steps: Inject SQL-like q/sort; use invalid enums
Expected: Safe handling / 422; align §9.6-A

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.8-B  Wire scrub + plain / rich sanitizers
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.8-006 | [BE] SanitizeRequestInput on API group (SECURITY_SANITIZE_REQUEST_INPUT
             default true) strips null bytes and C0 controls (except tab/LF/CR)
Steps: PUT field with "\0" and other C0 chars
Expected: Scrubbed before controller; feature test Pass

TC-9.8-007 | [BE] TrimStrings / ConvertEmptyStringsToNull remain enabled
Steps: Send "  " / ""
Expected: Trimmed / null as Laravel defaults

TC-9.8-008 | [BE] Plain-text fields use InputSanitizer::plain() — strip tags +
             trim (names, subjects, email template copy, etc.)
Steps: Submit name with <b>tags</b>
Expected: Tags stripped in stored value

TC-9.8-009 | [BE] Rich HTML fields use InputSanitizer::richHtml() → SafeHtml when
             SECURITY_SANITIZE_RICH_HTML=true — covers Terms/Privacy/Cookies,
             GC/SMC description & feedback, support tickets, WC section/
             change-request content, post/bundle descriptions
Steps: Inject <script> into each category (sample ≥1 per domain)
Expected: Script/handlers/javascript: stripped; unit/feature tests Pass

TC-9.8-010 | [BE] PageContentDefaults::sanitize (or equivalent) allowlists
             structured settings keys
Steps: PUT page-content with unknown/dangerous keys
Expected: Stripped/ignored

TC-9.8-011 | [BE] Paths/redirects use SafeFrontendRedirect (§9.4)
Steps: Align TC-9.4-017/018
Expected: Evil hosts rejected

TC-9.8-012 | [BE] Uploads gated by ScanUploadedFiles (§9.5) in addition to
             validate() file rules
Steps: Align TC-9.5-006–008; also validate() mimes
Expected: Both layers fire

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.8-C  Frontend complementary (not sufficient alone)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.8-013 | [FE] sanitizeRichText (DOMPurify) before dangerouslySetInnerHTML
             and on submit for legal-page editors / page copy helpers
Steps: Submit script-laden rich text from SPA
Expected: Client strips; server also strips (defence in depth)

TC-9.8-014 | [FE/BE] Disabling/bypassing client validation still yields server
             422 / sanitize — client MUST NOT be only gate
Steps: Direct API call with invalid/malicious payload
Expected: Server rejects or sanitizes

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.8-D  Related distinct + acceptance docs
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.8-015 | [PROCESS] Distinct from NFR-SEC-APP / FW / NAV / SEC monitoring
Steps: Index
Expected: VAL is validate+sanitize gate

TC-9.8-016 | [PROCESS] Documented in §9.8 + §8 checklist [x]; env keys in
             .env.example (SECURITY_SANITIZE_REQUEST_INPUT,
             SECURITY_SANITIZE_RICH_HTML)
Steps: Inspect docs/.env.example
Expected: Present

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.8-E  E2E
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.8-017 | [FE+BE] E2E: Privacy update with null-byte + script → scrubbed/
             sanitized store → API GET shows clean content → invalid length
             → 422; GC description script stripped; upload .php still rejected
             by FW after validate
Steps: Full path
Expected: Acceptance pack Pass

================================================================================
SECTION 9.9 — NFR-AVAIL Module disaster recovery and hub availability  [ACTIVE]
================================================================================
Requirement summary:
  Status: IMPLEMENTED (2026-10-16). Functional detail in §6B.1 and §6F;
  whole-hub backups remain §6B. This NFR is the availability statement.

  Statement: When Security Monitoring recognizes a disaster affecting a product
  module, capable operators SHALL roll back THAT MODULE’s data from a module
  snapshot — without restoring the entire hub or all code.

  Product fulfilment:
  A) Detection — Security Monitoring events / incidents (§6F).
  B) Recognition + module rollback — §6B.1 (secmon_manage_disaster_recovery).
  C) Whole-hub last resort — §6B hub backup restore (separate).
  D) Deploy package rollback — §1C prior release apply (separate).

  Explicit non-goals (v1):
  - Automated multi-region failover / hot standby
  - Treating SecMon resolve/close as data recovery
  - Whole-code restore as part of module DR

  Acceptance [x]: Documented §6B.1 + §9.9 + §8; module snapshot + rollback APIs
  and SecMon UI; distinct from hub backup restore and incident status-only.
  Acceptance [ ] Off-site copies of hub backups remain an ops process.

PRIMARY SUITES:
  §6B.1 — TC-6B.1-001 … (full module DR pack)
  §6F / §3.3f — detection + secmon_manage_disaster_recovery gates
  §6B — whole-hub backup restore (last resort; not module DR)

Actors:
  A   = Role WITH secmon_manage_disaster_recovery (+ security_monitoring ON)
  OPS = Hub backup / off-site ops
  PA  = power_admin (hub backups)

Layer tags: [BE] [FE] [PROCESS] [SEC]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9.9-A  Reuse primary functional suites
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

TC-9.9-001 | [PROCESS] Execute §6B.1 suite (TC-6B.1-001+) as core NFR-AVAIL
             module-DR verification
Steps: Mark Pass/Fail on §6B.1 pack
Expected: Recognize → snapshot → module-scoped rollback covered

TC-9.9-002 | [PROCESS] Execute §6F / §3.3f detection + DR capability gates
             (e.g. TC-3.3f-011, 039–040) as NFR-AVAIL detection path
Steps: Mark Pass/Fail
Expected: Incidents/events detect; DR APIs gated by cap

TC-9.9-003 | [PROCESS] Traceability to §9.9 acceptance bullets

Expected: Docs, APIs+UI, distinct from hub restore & status-only; ops off-site open

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

             module’s tables — not whole hub / other modules
Steps: Align TC-6B.1-028–032; verify unrelated tables unchanged
Expected: Module-scoped restore; recovery_status=rolled_back

TC-9.9-005 | [BE/FE] DR UI under Security Monitoring; requires
             secmon_manage_disaster_recovery (+ Functionality ON)
